#2854. Mavericks vs. Pacers Quoteboard: ‘We got it to the guys in their money spots, and they made plays’

The Dallas Mavericks (7-4) notched their second win of the young season, 125-122, over the Indiana Pacers (3-8) on Monday, and on the second night of a back-to-back set, no less. Heady stuff for the fifth-youngest roster in the NBA this year.

Cooper Flagg was sensational once again, finishing with 43 points, 10 rebounds and three steals, while Kyrie Irving added 35 points on seven made 3-pointers.

Let's see what the two superstars, and their coach, Dusty May, had to say in the postgame presser.

Very good win for us in the homestand. Our two guys, superstars, obviously made play after play after play. I thought, especially in the fourth quarter, we sat down and were able to contain the basketball a little bit better than we did the first three. Indiana, they're a tough cover. They rarely let you get your defense set. They put so much pressure on the paint, and then they play well together and spray it out. I thought their bench was incredible tonight for them. Just a good win against a great organization, a well-coached team, and a bunch of guys that we have a lot of respect for.

I mean, we got it to the guys in their money spots and they made plays. The Ky-Naji pick-and-roll, short roll — that short roll actually was really clicking in the first half with Caleb. When Caleb would go out, we weren't able to get to it as well. I thought Naji created the window. Ky made an excellent pass. Even the two possessions prior to that, we got to the rim. Coop got to the rim. Naji got to the rim. They both got there and made really good plays, and the ball just didn't drop. At that point, you try not to … overthink it. At that point, we were thinking about spreading the floor again and just trying to open up the driving lanes. We ran very simple actions to get Coop on the left side of the floor. Obviously, he just made it — did his thing.

He gets to the rim. I think him almost being a left-handed player in the paint certainly helps. He's a great left-footed jumper, left-handed finisher. Then he's able to square in the air from 15, 17 feet. He can just get to his spots. If there's a smaller guy, he can jump and just square in the air over them. The last couple of games, he's made some threes, and he's making all of his free throws. That's how you put up the 43 points, where you do a little bit of everything. He finished at the mid-range. He finished at the rim. He got to the line. He's getting to the line more than he has. He affects the game in so many ways, and he makes the right plays as far as passing. Thank goodness he's on our team. It's unbelievable that he's a 19-year-old doing this.

Yeah, sometimes I think the plus-minus in a one-game sample size is a little bit overblown. His plus-29 tonight was very indicative of how important he is. When we were struggling to make shots in the first half. We thought we were generating really good looks. We just looked a little bit flat. I thought Kyrie's shot-making just kind of righted the ship for us. We came out in the third quarter and were able to get over the hump a little bit. He makes tough shots. He's able to hang in the paint and finish. The same, just like Coop, they're so unselfish. They're such good teammates that winning is the priority over everything.

I thought our gap presentation was much better in his isolations. In the first half, it felt like we were close enough, but we weren't impacting the shot line. He was able just to raise up and shoot without us impacting the line of the shot. In the second half, I felt like we got underneath him, and we had much better gap help. Then really the guy on the ball did a good job, but also the other four. Then he was able to get into the mid-post. I thought our rotations were clean. We had doubled him from the baseline side early in the game, and he picked us apart. We came from the top side, and our rotations, other than one time, were pretty clean, and it worked out well for us.

You just see a guy that just continues to evolve and get better. He looks stronger. He looks more determined. When it's winning time, you just see he's impacting every possession, rebounding-wise. He made a couple defensive errors. I'm sure he'll be disappointed in that, and he's going to clean up. Just the way he impacts every possession. I think probably the mental fatigue of being so engaged is probably tougher than the physical fatigue for the 19-year-old. Those guys have to fight for every catch. They're at the top of every scouting report. For them to do that against a team like Indiana and have the cumulative effort that they did, it's impressive.

We got 10 [offensive rebounds] tonight, and that's actually much less than — I think we had 51 in the three games prior. Our offensive rebounding's really improved. Tonight, it didn't seem like — because we were playing a little bit different when we were trying to tackle some matchups and we were trying to get in the short roll, we didn't quite have our same rhythm and flow. Late in the game, we were just getting the ball to certain players. We didn't have cutting. Obviously, when Gaff plays less than 20 minutes, that's a big part of our offensive rebounding. And Rez, another one being out — that's probably the difference in us averaging 16 and getting 10. It's probably just a personnel deal.

How well they complement each other and how committed they are to simply trying to find a way to win, whatever this game needs. It's a unique deal. We're coming out of a timeout, we may draw up a play for one of them just because the other one might have an All-League defender on him, or whatever the case, and they never roll their eyes. They know when we're going to one of the two, the other one's very, very supportive because they have that much respect for each other. They're both just such good guys and great teammates.

Yeah, I think that's something I've talked a lot about, but just having him out there, his gravity opening the floor for everybody else — it makes my life so much easier. That's that, but I think it speaks to our team as well. The other night, PJ had 21 in the first half. He had it going. Gaff had it going the other night. Every night is going to be somebody else's night. We know that that's our identity, that we're making the right plays, no matter what, we're trusting each other. Tonight, me and Kyrie had it going. There's hopefully going to be a lot of nights like that where we're able to do our thing. It's a next-man-up mentality. No Rez tonight. I thought Santi stepped up —played really, really well. I think it's just the identity of our team — just making the right plays and everybody having that trust in each other.

I think just the unselfishness of everybody out there. Having someone like Walt [Clayton Jr.] out there too — the way he's able to get downhill and help us initiate offense. Naji. We get lineups out there, and we just get the ball moving at a fast level. We have guys who know where they should shoot their shots — where the other people are involved. It clicks at a really high level. I just thought it looked really good tonight, moving around, swinging, finding the right shots.

Obviously, we feel good. I think even some of the losses, we felt like we learned a lot from. Something we talked about after our last loss is that we're not always going to be able to learn from the wins. Games like tonight, where you're able to execute down the stretch — every experience, good or bad, win or loss, I think just learning from everything. We have a younger group, still with some really good older veterans. I think just being able to learn on the fly and take a lot and dissect from each and every one of these games is going to be big for us.

I think just being able to get to my spots at a higher level and being more comfortable with that has allowed me to take a little bit of load off my body. Just kind of stopping short, raising up over people. It's taxing going downhill all the way to the rim and trying to finish over seven-footers all night, every night. I think just adding that in there definitely allows me to pace myself a little bit, steal some easy ones. Then again, just trusting the work, trusting the spot work that I put in all summer long. Just being so comfortable with those shots. I think it goes back to my teammates, who are just trusting me with make or miss, getting to those spots. When I'm in that mindset that no matter what, as long as I'm getting the shot that I'm comfortable with, that is going to be a good outcome for our team.

Like I said, just all the work all summer long. Just preparing myself to be in those moments down the stretch. Limited space, limited dribbles. Being able to come out with a bucket and a good, comfortable shot.

Yeah, I think it just speaks to the type of basketball that I've always played and how I was raised, just being a true competitor. I think whenever I get out there on the court, it just goes back to being a competitor and trying to make as many possible winning plays as I can, and trying to make the easy, right basketball play out there. Just speaks to that. My teammates are putting me in good positions. My coaches are trusting me through make or miss.

Yeah, we definitely are having a lot of fun, including with all our teammates and just the support we give each other. I think, like you said, I played with a few other great players in the league. I had the luxury of watching them up close, being in workouts, being in some high-level games, winning a lot of games, losing a lot of games. So just learning on the fly, I think that's key, and just enjoying the process. I feel like that's what we're doing. We keep calling him a teenager, but he plays way beyond his years. We've got to give him his flowers. Just the way he's always in attack mode, or the way he sees the game, and he's always getting others involved — very selfless. I think it helps out our team a lot, including myself, just seeing him be able to make plays for others. But also when he's calling his own number, he's getting in that paint and trying to get the most efficient shot possible. Most young guys will probably move away from that contact, but it seems like this newer generation that's coming into our league, they fear nothing. And I think he falls right in line with that. So grateful to have him on the wing with me, and just consistently telling each other that there's another level we can get to and we can keep pushing and keep getting better. So that's a great, great thing that we have going right now.

I won't divulge too much information on the injury management plan because I could get my words twisted. But I would say that just in terms of our medical staff and the connection that we have, and just the build-up process over the last month — the minutes restriction, just trying to get in better conditioning, better shape, basketball shape, playing against the best players in the world. When you're in a combative sport such as basketball, some days are going to feel good, some days aren't, especially coming off an injury or a long period of time. So I just had to get my body back used to being in the flow of the game, and just after games, what does my recovery look like? And I was just really excited after last night's game. We handled business in the first half. We came out in the second half, handled business again. I played 29 minutes. And I was just feeling great, and just coming into today — last night, I keep jumping around — but last night, just letting the guys know in the locker room right after the game, like, I plan on playing. So it was just telling our team, and just being prepared for whatever the Indiana Pacers were going to throw at us, and we're going to be there together. So obviously we didn't have Rez tonight. We still have a few guys out, but we have guys returning. And I think it's very important for me to be prepared whether I'm playing or not in the back-to-back, but I feel like my attitude was just in the right place to be able to be prepared for this game tonight. So grateful.

I mean, everybody's different. You know, everybody's greatness looks different. Everybody's attitude kind of looks different. But I think, you know, the telltale sign is just when you have the mutual respect of the guy that's next to you, or the guys that are on your team — you want to see them do well. And I think that's a trait that I've seen in playing with a lot of great players — the selflessness we talk about, of letting someone else go get it, or letting someone else get in their rhythm, or celebrating your teammates' success as well, or sacrificing yourself in the possession to be able to get someone else a better shot. This is very important. And when someone exudes those types of traits, especially when they're an offensive, all-world talent, it just does something for the group. And then also when we're able to do it on the defensive end, where we're making our identity, trying to limit teams to one possession and one shot, and crash the offensive glass, and just do the little things that get us wins — you know, probably not the prettiest team. You know what I mean? We need to make sure that we're doing all of the little things. And I feel like we're trending in the right direction. So me playing with Cooper, you know, playing with other superstars, playing with other guys, I don't really like to compare them. It's just the unique [unclear], or just the greatness that they try to display, that I try to complement, and vice versa.

I think it's a multitude of things. I think last year we saw signs of it. You know, had a few high-scoring games down the stretch. The ball was in his hands. We came up short a few times, but we did win a few games down the stretch. And I think me watching from the sideline, I saw where I can implement myself to help him out, and then also seeing the positions where he naturally puts himself in, where he can help me out. And he's definitely a hard worker. He puts in the time, and you deserve to be rewarded for that. And just coming off Rookie of the Year, a lot of pressure on him. And my goal as a veteran is to alleviate a lot of that pressure that he feels and just be ready to give what the game needs. So I think you heard Dusty say it, as well as Coop say it over the last few weeks — you know, I think the onus is on us, and, you know, eyes are on us all the time. The gravity's always coming towards us. But for us, we all understand that we're not going to be as successful unless all of us are galvanizing each other. And it starts with me and Coop, but obviously it leads into other avenues that other guys can take advantage of. So we're just trying to put ourselves in the right positions out there and have fun playing basketball. And I think when you see us displaying that, it's us having fun, and it starts on the defensive end. And then when you see us getting out in transition, it's just a beautiful game to watch, because he makes the game easy, I make the game easy. Had a few turnovers, you know, over the past few games that we can definitely avoid. And even I think back to just the San Antonio game — you know, these past few games, just the way that we're finishing strong and we're able to make a lot of nonverbal cues, get in the right position down the stretch, I think we'll be better prepared moving forward. So we needed that San Antonio game to learn a lot of lessons. I took a lot of bad shots in that game as well down the stretch, where I felt like I settled, and rewatched a lot of the film, see where I can get better, and try to vocalize that to my teammates as well. So they trust me with the ball in my hands, but also when I give them the ball. And I'm going to keep saying it starts with me and Coop, but obviously we're nothing without our group and our coaching staff and everybody believing in what we have going on here.

Santi's a growing veteran in this league. And anytime you're coming back from injury — I think his first basket was a layup, I believe — you know, so just anytime you can get yourself going and getting back into the rhythm, and you're coming back in the beginning of the season, it could feel a little awkward — your body could feel a little bit out of rhythm. But it's our job to make the game easy. And he can use our gravity to his advantage. And he's done such a great job of being a spacer for the previous team he played on, Memphis, and being able to be available as a four-five mix, but also play the three spot. And if we do go small, it's just diversifying, I feel like, the lineups that we can have out there. And he's very complementary to what we're doing and what we're building. So I'm grateful that we can have him out there. But it also starts with our pace and our point of attack, and being able to get downhill, being able to create other opportunities when we know defenses are geared towards us.

I think we're smart about our motivation and what motivates us in the locker room. I think we heard a lot of the noise before the season and some of the predictions, and I'm not going to say anybody wrote us off, because it's not that serious. I just think because of the roster that we had, and no one really sure about where I was going to be or where our team was going to be, or some of the trade rumors that continue to circle around our team or circle me — just distractions. And I think we just laid it right there, and I let the team know out of my own mouth that these trade rumors, anything that's going on with me, do not let this infiltrate our locker room. Don't let this get in the way of what we have building here. We have a lot of depth, we have a lot of youth, and we should use that to our advantage, but we should not allow any external distractions or any external voices that don't support us unconditionally or want to see us do well to infiltrate our locker room. So I think that's one of the contributing factors. But I think another one is Dusty's doing a great job of confronting us when we're not doing things right, where we're not living up to our standards, and he knows that we're better when we're hunting, and that's what we are. We haven't earned anything in this league yet. We consistently do it every night, and that's how you build your identity in this league and you earn your respect. We try not to put the cart before the horse, in a sense, and we just try to take it slow, and when we're preparing for games, prepare for it as though we're getting ready for the playoffs or a great team, and give everybody the same respect and go out there and play our best basketball.

#2850. Amazon's Ring unveils flying drone and robot to patrol homes and businesses

Amazon's Ring is going deeper into surveillance with an indoor drone for home monitoring and an autonomous robot designed to patrol businesses.

The company on Tuesday unveiled the $250 Ring Drone Cam, a security camera that can fly from room to room, including upstairs, as well as capture footage during the day or at night. When an alarm or sensor on windows, doors and home security devices is triggered, the textbook-sized drone travels a preset path to investigate suspicious activity. Users can also deploy the drone at any time, the company said.

At a demo in New York on Monday, Ring CEO Jamie Siminoff said the drone could be used as a home monitoring tool and as a crime "deterrent" due to the device's built-in LED lights.

"If someone is going around your house at night, and your motion detector goes off on your Ring doorbell, imagine if you see spotlights and something flying across the window," Siminoff said. "You're probably not going to go into that house."

The drone, which can fly up to eight minutes at a time before autonomously returning to its charging dock, has two plastic-encased propellers that emit a loud buzz when it's airborne.

Ring devices have provided Amazon another avenue to grow its presence in the smart home beyond its Echo smart speakers and Eero routers, expanding in a market that Grand View Research estimates will top $200 billion in sales this year. It's equally fertile ground for Amazon rivals Apple and Google.

Ring first previewed its drone, then called the Always Home Cam, in 2020. Siminoff said he chose to hold off on releasing the device until Amazon could perfect the design, sensors and data processing.

The company on Tuesday also debuted the $1,000 Ring Rover Cam. The device repurposes Amazon's Astro home robot, which had a short life as a business device and has seen few upgrades since its 2021 debut, for companies to use to patrol offices, warehouses and other workspaces.

Ring said it "leveraged the best of Astro" to build the Ring Rover Cam, which it describes as a "purpose-built security robot."

Similar to the Astro, the device is a display mounted on a two-wheeled base with an extendable periscope camera. It also features a "built-in siren," two-way audio and a "light tower" that shines onto the ceiling to help users keep track of its whereabouts.

Siminoff said in an interview that the drone and the robot allow users to monitor multiple areas of their home or business with just one device.

"People like indoor cameras, but they don't necessarily want them everywhere around their home," Siminoff said.

Siminoff said the drone can't fly outside per Federal Aviation Administration regulations, which restrict operating drones unless they're within a line of sight of a pilot.

"We have told our customers not to do that because it's illegal," Siminoff said. "We've also put guardrails in there to make it so it doesn't fly outside."

The Ring Drone Cam ships in the U.S. on Jan. 27, pending approval from the Federal Communications Commission, while the Ring Rover Cam will begin shipping to users on Dec. 16, the company said.

#2849. Frustrated Jordan Poole open to Bay Area return after 51-point loss to Warriors

SAN FRANCISCO ― Jordan Poole helped the Golden State Warriors win an NBA championship in 2022, but it hasn't always gone well for Poole since leaving the Bay Area via trade in 2023.

That was certainly true in his return Monday night.

The Warriors delivered a 51-point beatdown Nov. 9 against the Pelicans, defeating them 138-87 at Chase Center. After the game, Poole sounded off after his Pelicans dropped to 4-7.

"Pretty (expletive) difficult. Pretty (expletive) difficult," Poole told reporters. He also reminisced about better days in the Bay Area.

"I mean you just played right. They played the right way, bro. They played good basketball. They played team basketball," Poole said. "Obviously, the environment is pretty high level. But like I said, once you put a lot of work and you got hardware with an organization, with a team, with a city, you're kind of cemented in the history. So it's good, but do I miss it? Hell yeah!"

Poole, who finished with seven points on 1-of-5 field goals with three rebounds and two assists, is averaging just 6.2 points on 19% shooting from the field and 23.5% on 3-point attempts.

That's a long way from his best season with Golden State during 2022-23, when he averaged 20.4 points.

Poole, who had a reunion with former backcourt mate Stephen Curry before tip-off, said after the game that he'd absolutely welcome an official reunion back to the Bay.

"Yes. I mean, it feels like home, though," Poole said. "Driving into the (expletive) arena, you know, crossing the the Bay Bridge or wherever you're coming from, it definitely feels natural. And I think it's pretty evident, you know, the way that I play is very fitting to how I used to play here. Nothing's changed, you know, other than the system that I'm in.

"We won a championship, you know, so it works. And it did work," he added. "That's what you ultimately want to do when you come out here and you play the game. So, to answer your question, definitely. This is my last year on the deal (in New Orleans). I'm a free agent at the end of the season, so you never really know what's gonna happen."

Such a return might mean reuniting with Draymond Green. A leaked video in 2022 showed Green punch Poole during a practice that season. The Warriors traded Poole the following year. Green, who is also in the final year of his contract, wasn't opposed to a reunion.

"That would be incredible," Green said according to the Mercury News. "I have no issue with that at all. I will never stand in the way of this organization thinking they can better themselves. The issue will never be me. Ever."

Poole is in the final year of a four-year, $128 million contract, he signed with the Warriors before being traded to the Wizards. Poole is owed $34 million this season and then he will become an unrestricted free agent in the summer of 2027. He said if there's any place he's comfortable playing, it's where it all started in the Bay Area.

"I always love coming back to the Bay. Definitely feels like home," Poole said. "The fans are always really welcoming. Always cool to see some jerseys and fans I used to see her all the time, so it's definitely a warm welcome, and I definitely miss it for sure."

Poole was drafted by the Warriors with the 28th overall pick in the 2019 NBA Draft, becoming a champion and a fan favorite in the Bay Area.

The Pelicans' last winning season was the 2023–24 season, when they finished with a record of 49–33. Poole, then, played for the Washington Wizards, who finished 15–67.

"It's only so much that you can control. You know what I mean?" Poole said. "I think over the last probably last few seasons, I think I've had five coaches in four years, and it's a tough recipe to be successful. And different organizations trying to figure out what they want to do with the team. Multiple front offices, so it's just been a lot of moving parts."

A lot of Poole's frustration stems from leaving a winning culture with the Warriors for franchises struggling to find their identity.

"Coming from a team that had one common goal, knew we wanted to win. Everybody's on the same page. Everybody came into camp trying to win a championship, ready to sacrifice playing team ball," Poole said. "That's kind of like the way I grew up; was the way I played my entire career. So it's definitely different, and it's been an adjustment. But there's nothing better than playing the right way and playing team ball.

"I think tonight, just watching the game was a prime example of that," he added. "I mean, it's been pretty difficult, but you know, that's life. You have to just figure out ways to keep pushing, keep thriving, and you know, try to make the most of whatever situation you have. Keep learning."

Poole and Warriors rookie Yaxel Lendeborg are both former Michigan Wolverines, and the two shared a moment after the game, swapping jerseys.

"We share the same alma mater. He got a championship, I told him congrats on that. We like to keep our Michigan circle pretty close-knit," Poole said. "Let him know that he has another supporter in the league. He's been playing really well and tremendous. I just want him to keep it up man. If there's anything that he needs or anything I can do to help, you know, just being a guy who's been in the league for a little bit, I just told him to reach out."

"It was amazing. I've always wanted to meet him since I went to Michigan," Lendeborg said to USA TODAY Sports about Poole. "Hearing, seeing everything he did here for Golden State, he's an phenomenal player, phenomenal person. He seems like a guy who pretty much has the same personality and character as me. It was great to meet him, he said a bunch of kind words. It was a great interaction for me."

#2848. Fannie Mae, Freddie Mac rally as Ackman presses for conservatorship exit

The midterm elections could be a turning point for the release of Fannie Mae and Freddie Mac from government control, according to billionaire investor Bill Ackman.

Investors may agree with him, if the rally in both stocks is any sign.

The Federal National Mortgage Association, known as Fannie Mae, and Federal Home Loan Mortgage Corporation, or Freddie Mac, have each jumped more than 60% from Oct. 14 to Monday's close. The stocks were both up roughly 3% on Tuesday.

Ackman, the founder of Pershing Square Capital Management, has long pushed for the two entities to be released from federal conservatorship.

"The reason why it's not being touched right now is, you know, midterms," he said in an interview with CNBC's "Squawk Box."

Ackman, who owns both names through Pershing Square, believes Freddie and Fannie have the potential to increase "10-fold from current share prices," he said in his quarterly letter to shareholders, issued on Monday.

Fannie and Freddie buy mortgages from banks and other lenders, and then package them into mortgage-backed securities, known as MBS. Their operations are critical to the housing market, providing liquidity to banks and creating a secondary market for MBS.

The entities were placed into conservatorship in September 2008, during the financial crisis. The stocks were delisted from the New York Stock Exchange in 2010 and have traded over the counter since then.

Ackman wants the conservatorship to end, arguing the entities would be worth multiples of their current valuation if they were freed from government control.

President Donald Trump has supported ending the conservatorship of Fannie and Freddie. But it remains politically and economically contentious.

The entities operate under strict capital threshold requirements and would need to be recapitalized, which could happen through issuing common equity. However, the Treasury Department would need to figure out an exit plan for its roughly $388 billion ownership stake in the entities through preferred securities and warrants.

Congress may also need to play a role, passing legislation to restructure the government's role in mortgage financing and home-loan guarantees.

Nonetheless, Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent recently said it could happen before Trump's term ends, telling 2Way's "The Morning Meeting" podcast that the administration will tackle the ownership structure "next year, the year after." He said affordability will be guiding the decision.

One point of controversy is whether ending the conservatorship of Fannie and Freddie would increase the spreads on MBS over Treasuries, effectively making mortgages costlier. Bessent has sought to reassure the market that wouldn't happen.

"Whatever we do is going to be guided by making sure that the spread on [agency] mortgages … over the Treasury does not increase," Bessent said.

Ackman argues that privatizing the GSEs would help insulate them from political meddling. "Fannie and Freddie are these massive, enormously profitable institutions where whichever political party controls them…if they're still in conservatorship, can influence the outcome of elections," he told CNBC.

In one scenario, he explained, Democrats win the White House, conservatorship is still in place, and Democrats push Fannie and Freddie to lower credit standards in order to expand home ownership and affordability.

"If they're going to move more aggressively into borrowers with lower credit quality, for example, to achieve a political objective, that's going to have an impact on trillions of dollars outstanding mortgage securities," Ackman said. "It's going to have an impact on overall credit spreads."

MBS investors want certainty about how the entities will be run, he noted.

"That only gets resolved in a world in which they exit conservatorship, they become publicly traded companies that are owned by institutional and retail investors," Ackman said. "We set the rules. In conservatorship, the president can do anything."

#2846. Credit card debt climbs to a record $1.29 trillion, New York Fed research finds

Americans are increasingly turning to credit cards as financial pressures mount, new reports show.

Collectively, credit card balances climbed by $22 billion in the third quarter to a record $1.29 trillion, according to the Federal Reserve Bank of New York's latest household debt report, released Tuesday. Balances are up 4.2% from a year earlier.

Total household debt, including mortgages, auto loans and student loans, also expanded by $334 billion for the July-to-September period to a record $19.1 trillion.

"Overall debt has been growing," the New York Fed researchers said on a press call Tuesday. "This does reflect financial hardship out there."

The average credit card balance per consumer now stands at $6,678, up 2.4% year over year, a separate quarterly credit industry insights report from TransUnion found. The number of people with credit cards rose 2% to 178 million from 174.8 million.

Consumers often turn to credit cards in part because they are more accessible than other types of loans — even though the average interest rate on a new credit card offer is about 24%, according to data from LendingTree. Credit card APRs vary depending on the type of card, the issuer and the borrower's creditworthiness.

"Credit cards continue to be one of the most expensive ways to borrow," said Charlie Wise, TransUnion's senior vice president of global research and consulting.

Over time, an increasing share of new credit cards has gone to borrowers with lower incomes and credit scores, and "those accounts are now showing higher levels of distress," the New York Fed researchers said in a corresponding blog post.

Credit card issuers loosened their lending standards during the Covid pandemic, and delinquency rates rose in the years that followed, the New York Fed researchers said on the press call.

"Macroeconomic headwinds — namely high inflation and rising borrowing costs — have squeezed consumers, with inflation outpacing wage growth for low-income brackets," according to the New York Fed.

Meanwhile, the cost of living continues to climb, with Americans paying more for essentials such as groceries and gas.

Stubborn inflation has now stretched on for years, putting many households in an affordability squeeze. Costs for goods and services rose more than expected again in October, the Bureau of Labor Statistics also reported Tuesday.

"Higher prices and higher rates have pushed more people into debt," said Ted Rossman, principal consumer finance analyst at Money Management International, a nonprofit credit counseling agency.

Balances have continued to swell in the wake of the pandemic, and even though the growth rate is now slowing, "from a household finance standpoint, a lot of damage has been done," Rossman said.

The reports come on the heels of the Democrats' strong midterm results, when many voters expressed their dissatisfaction with the U.S. economy, according to Kyle Moore, chief economist at the nonpartisan think tank The Century Foundation.

"The throughline connecting so many of Tuesday's outcomes was a recognition that the American economy is failing American families," Moore said, referring to Election Day.

#2845. Dolphins add QB Malik Willis to IR due to lingering groin injury; Kyle McCord expected to start vs. Colts

The Miami Dolphins announced Tuesday that they are placing quarterback Malik Willis on injured reserve.

Willis has had a lingering groin injury that has been impacting his mobility. The quarterback had an MRI, and it showed that it is now a core muscle injury. Willis doesn't need surgery, but the amount of rest required to recover necessitated an IR stint.

With Willis sidelined for at least the next month, backup quarterback Kyle McCord is expected to take over as the starter this weekend against the Indianapolis Colts, per ESPN's Adam Schefter. Willis is not expected to miss the remainder of the season.

Willis joined the Dolphins this offseason, signing a 3-year, $67.5 million deal in free agency. He reunited with new Miami head coach Jeff Hafley, who was with Willis last year in Green Bay as the defensive coordinator.

Willis has played in eight games this season and has thrown for 1,396 yards, 6 touchdowns and 6 interceptions.

The 27-year-old is known for being able to use his legs and has picked up 232 yards and three additional scores on the ground. Willis is currently Miami's second-leading rusher behind Ollie Gordon (463 yards, 4 TDs).

McCord was a sixth-round pick in the 2025 NFL Draft by the Philadelphia Eagles. He spent the season on Philadelphia's practice squad, but joined the Packers in the offseason on a reserve/futures contract.

The quarterback was then traded to the Dolphins in late August for a 2028 sixth-round pick to replace Quinn Ewers, whom Miami traded to the Jacksonville Jaguars.

McCord has yet to play a snap in his pro career, but that is expected to change Sunday against the Colts, as he is in line to make his first career start.

Brady Cook is currently Miami's only other quarterback, and he is on the practice squad.

#2844. Jensen Huang's affair with alleged Chinese spy further complicates Nvidia’s China problem

Following Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's admission on Sunday to an extramarital affair with alleged Chinese spy Alice Huang, the chipmaker's relationship to China is about to come under intense scrutiny in Washington, D.C.

Since October 2022, when the Biden administration announced sweeping controls to limit China's ability to obtain advanced U.S. chips, Nvidia has seen its business diminish in the world's second-largest economy. Months after an even stricter set of rules was implemented by the Trump administration, Huang said in October 2025, "We are 100% out of China."

The recent allegations against Huang, while throwing the chipmaker into crisis, have also brought attention to the Nvidia CEO's potential influence over U.S. policy on chip sales to China, where its business once flourished. Of particular interest is whether restricted Nvidia chips have reached Chinese companies through customers like Singapore-based Megaspeed, the company linked to Alice Huang.

The New York Times first reported that the Chinese businesswoman was a spy for China's Ministry of State Security, according to people briefed on the intelligence circulated in a memo among the Trump administration in the spring. China has called the allegation a smear.

Jensen Huang has been closely aligned with President Donald Trump during his second administration, helping advise the White House on artificial intelligence safety regulations, while also pushing for looser restrictions on selling AI chips to China. In December, Trump said Nvidia would be allowed to ship its H200 chips to China, provided the U.S. got a 25% cut of sales. In March, Huang told reporters that Nvidia had "received purchase orders, and we're in the process of restarting our manufacturing."

Huang was a last-minute addition to the roster of tech executives who joined Trump on a trip to China in May, after the president was briefed on intelligence concerns, according to the Times.

"In 35 years, I haven't seen a chip CEO with this much direct influence on export policy," said Patrick Moorhead, CEO at Moor Insights & Strategy, in an email.

Since last week's midterm elections that included sweeping victories across the country for Democrats, the mood in Washington has changed. Three House committees have requested closed-door briefings with the Nvidia CEO about his relationship with Megaspeed.

Democratic lawmakers, who are about to take power in the House and Senate, have said publicly that Congress will investigate and seek answers. Trump said Monday that he was "amazed" by the allegations and that Huang told him he "had no idea that she was a spy." The FBI is now looking into the issue, Trump said at a White House briefing.

It's been a long four years for Nvidia in China.

Before the initial export controls in 2022, China made up nearly a quarter of Nvidia's revenue. Nvidia responded to the Biden-era restrictions by redesigning some of its products to comply with the new regulations.

"It was a turbulent month or so as the company went upside down to re-engineer" the products, Huang told CNBC in a 2023 interview. Still, he was confident the company could continue selling chips to China. "I think we're going to be just fine in the ability to serve the customers there," he said.

After the Biden administration tightened export restrictions again, Nvidia redesigned another AI graphics processing unit for China, the H20. Following reports that Chinese model developer DeepSeek was using restricted Nvidia chips, the Trump administration limited sales of H20. Nvidia revealed it would incur $5.5 billion in costs related to canceled orders for the redesigned chip.

However, despite saying in its forecasts that "we're assuming 0% for China," the company maintained business in the region. In the last fiscal year, Nvidia reported that about 9% of its revenue came from China, including Hong Kong, although that includes revenue from segments outside of restricted data center chips, such as gaming, auto and industrial. Nvidia's China revenue was 8% in the most recent quarter.

A report in May from short seller Culper Research alleged Nvidia's China exposure was much higher. The firm said that more than 20% of Nvidia's fiscal 2026 compute revenue was driven by China, supported by "illegal GPU diversion and Southeast Asian intermediaries," according to the report.

A loophole allows Chinese companies to rent Nvidia GPUs in data centers, even when they're restricted from buying them outright. For example, a Chinese company could lease Nvidia computing power in Europe by renting server capacity from a non-Chinese neocloud or data center operator that's exempt from export controls.

Nvidia didn't respond to a request for comment for this story.

Megaspeed, an Nvidia customer based in Singapore, has been accused of diverting its own supply of Nvidia's restricted AI chips to Chinese companies. Megaspeed denies wrongdoing and said inspections found no evidence of violations.

In 2025, Nvidia changed the way it reported regional sales to categorize by customer headquarters, instead of by where the products were billed. Nvidia also stopped reporting Singapore sales during the same period.

"Whether Blackwell chips reached China is a Megaspeed question that predates this week," said Moorhead, pointing to Bloomberg reporting in December 2025 that found Megaspeed had imported 50,000 more Nvidia chips than Nvidia tallied in its data centers.

In 2025, a House Select Committee opened an investigation into whether China's DeepSeek operates on Nvidia's chips.

A separate investigation by the U.S. Commerce Department was centered on Megaspeed. That probe was abruptly transferred to the Justice Department, the Times reported, following an email from Nvidia's general counsel to the Commerce Department seeking information about the investigation.

The restrictions aren't just coming from the U.S. China has continued to limit the amount of Nvidia chips it will allow into the country, keeping Nvidia's China sales numbers down. The U.S. has licensed H200 sales to Alibaba, Tencent, ByteDance and other Chinese companies, but Beijing hasn't approved large-scale imports, Moorhead told CNBC. Nvidia said H200 shipments to China made up less than 1% of data center revenue for the quarter that ended in July.

Moorhead expects more export control legislation to pass as a result of the latest turmoil. Proposals could allow Congress oversight of chip export licenses or require chipmakers to install security mechanisms to track the location of chips to determine if they've found their way to China.

"Every China license now gets read through this story," Moorhead said. "Jensen has been Nvidia's lead voice on China, and that voice is weaker."

— CNBC's Kif Leswing and Seema Mody contributed reporting to this report.

Correction: Three House committees have requested closed-door briefings with the Nvidia CEO about his relationship with Megaspeed. An earlier version misstated the time element.

#2843. Germany U21 ‘television tower’ keeper Tom Ritzy Hülsmann leaves teammates looking up

Germany U21's newest goalkeeper Tom Ritzy Hülsmann has already made quite an impression on his international teammates. The 22-year-old Stade Reims shot-stopper stands an imposing 2.05 metres tall, and his first encounters with fellow internationals Muhammed Damar and Maurice Krattenmacher have provided some entertaining stories.

Speaking at Tuesday's DFB U21 press conference, attended remotely by Get German Football News, the two midfielders offered amusing accounts of their introductions to the towering goalkeeper. Hülsmann, a former Bayern Munich youth prospect, received his first call-up to any German national team when head coach Antonio Di Salvo announced his squad for November's internationals against Belgium and Croatia.

The Trier native spent last season with Austrian Bundesliga outfit TSV Hartberg, where he was voted the league's best goalkeeper. His performances earned him a summer transfer to French second-division side Stade Reims. Hülsmann's impressive physical stature was the subject of some lighthearted discussion as Tuesday's press conference drew to a close.

DFB U21 press spokesperson Markus Beer brought up the subject of Germany's towering new goalkeeper, noting that his arrival had apparently produced some rather unusual situations when the players first greeted him.

"He's like a television tower," the Wolfsburg midfielder explained. "When I first saw him, I asked him what tablets he takes. He told me it's just genetics. Apparently, his parents are very tall too."

The 22-year-old continued with an amusing account of their initial handshake.

"When he stood in front of me and wanted to shake my hand, I had to look up three storeys," Damar said. "I thought [Joshua] Quarshie was already tall, but compared to him, he's an ant."

Damar's reference was to fellow Germany U21 defender Joshua Quarshie, another player who possesses an imposing physical stature.

Elversberg midfielder Maurice Krattenmacher, who earned a late call-up to the squad following several injury-related withdrawals, was similarly amused by his first encounter with Hülsmann.

"I can only agree," Krattenmacher said. "You've got a huge tower coming towards you. But he's a great guy, and I'm looking forward to training with him."

Beer acknowledged that Hülsmann's height had indeed produced some peculiar moments during the team's initial introductions, bringing the press conference to an entertaining conclusion.

The amusing exchange provided some levity at the end of a press conference otherwise focused on Germany's preparations for next summer's European U21 Championship.

Earlier in proceedings, Beer had briefly intervened to emphasize that the upcoming encounters with Belgium and Croatia should be regarded as preparation matches for the European Championship rather than simply friendlies.

Germany have already secured qualification for next summer's tournament in Albania and Serbia, with the November internationals providing Di Salvo with an opportunity to integrate new players and evaluate his team's progress against fellow European Championship participants.

The Germans host Belgium at the Stadion an der Hafenstraße in Essen on Friday, November 13, before travelling to Rijeka to face Croatia four days later.

Hülsmann will be hoping to make his first international appearance during the upcoming fixtures. Whatever happens on the pitch, the Stade Reims goalkeeper has certainly already made his presence felt among his new teammates.

#2842. Homeowners tap equity as HELOC balances post biggest jump since 2005: New York Fed report

Homeowners tapped their home equity lines of credit in the third quarter at the highest rate in more than two decades, according to a new Federal Reserve Bank of New York report on household debt released Tuesday.

Balances on HELOCs jumped by $23 billion during the three months ending Sept. 30, the largest increase since 2005. That brings the outstanding amount owed to $481 billion, the report shows. This also marks the 18th consecutive quarterly increase.

HELOC balances peaked at roughly $714 billion in the first quarter of 2009, according to a 2015 New York Fed report.

The current rise in balances comes amid a sluggish housing market, due at least partly to the so-called mortgage rate lock-in effect. Just under half of outstanding mortgages — 49.1% — carried rates of 4% or lower in the second quarter, according to a Realtor.com analysis of federal mortgage data, leaving many owners reluctant to give up their low rates to purchase another home at current higher borrowing costs.

The median price of an existing home was $417,500 in September, according to the National Association of Realtors, a real estate trade group. While up just 1.3% from a year earlier, the amount is nearly 54% higher than in September 2019, according to NAR. The average rate on a typical 30-year fixed-rate mortgage is 7.3% as of Tuesday, according to Mortgage News Daily.

"HELOCs let them borrow against their equity without giving up the low interest rate on their home," said certified financial planner Douglas Boneparth, president and founder of Bone Fide Wealth in New York City and a member of CNBC's Financial Advisor Council.

Mortgage holders collectively had $17.5 trillion in home equity as of the third quarter, according to a new report from Intercontinental Exchange, or ICE, a financial markets technology and data company.

Tappable equity that can be accessed — meaning a homeowner's equity would remain at or above 20% of the home's value after tapping it — rose 1.2%, or $16,600, from a year ago to an average of $204,000 per borrower, the ICE report shows.

"Homeowners are sitting on substantial housing wealth, with 2.4 million mortgage holders now holding at least $1 million in equity," Andy Walden, head of mortgage and Housing Market Research at ICE, said in the report.

HELOCs let you tap a line of credit over time as you need the money, instead of getting it all at once like a home equity loan or cash-out refinance.

While HELOCs may have fewer up-front costs than a home equity loan, they generally have a variable interest rate. This means it will move up and down based on a benchmark like the prime rate, which banks use as a basis to set rates on a variety of loans. And, while the Federal Reserve doesn't control that rate, it is influenced by changes the Fed makes to the so-called federal funds rate.

"If your budget only works at today's rate, then your budget doesn't really work," Boneparth said of HELOCs. "You kind of have to be mindful of the fact that rates may go up."

The average interest rate for a $30,000 HELOC was 7.49% as of Nov. 4, according to Bankrate.

Many HELOCs also have a "draw" period when you can take money out, which often lasts five or 10 years. During that time, you typically are only required to pay the interest on any funds you've withdrawn. After that, however, you'll enter a repayment period of, say, 10 or 20 years, when you're required to pay both interest and principal. Because of that, your payments will jump if you've only been paying interest.

"The real test comes when the repayment period kicks in, and the principal starts to show up, if that's how their HELOC is structured," Boneparth said. "Payments potentially could double overnight, and you really want to know that before you sign anything."

Additionally, consider what you're planning to use the money for, Boneparth said.

"If you're borrowing against your home to improve your home, that could make a lot of sense," Boneparth said. "Borrowing against your house to pay off credit cards is a little trickier because you're turning unsecured debt into debt secured by the roof over your head."

In that case, if your spending habits don't change, he said, "you're just moving the problem somewhere a little scarier."

#2840. Binance breach puts custody at center of crypto infrastructure trade

The $6.3 billion Binance hack could accelerate a shift in how investors value cryptocurrency companies, putting a greater premium on custody providers and the infrastructure needed to securely hold and protect digital assets.

Much of crypto's evolution this year has involved companies moving past their reliance on trading volumes and transaction fees, to focus instead on building the infrastructure connecting digital assets with traditional finance. Last Thursday's breach adds a new dimension, bringing cybersecurity, custody, compliance and balance-sheet resilience into sharper focus.

Importantly, the Binance breach was not an indictment of crypto itself, or a case of leadership-driven fraud, as with FTX in late 2022. Instead, Binance's leadership moved quickly to contain the fallout, protect customers and commit the exchange's own resources to cover losses.

On Tuesday, Binance CEO Richard Teng told CNBC's "Squawk on the Street" that the company has replenished 98% of $6.34 billion in affected assets, and that it is still in the process of purchasing outstanding, more illiquid assets.

Management's response, alongside efforts elsewhere in the industry to boost confidence, helped prevent a security breach from escalating into a broader crisis of confidence. For example, Tether, Circle, Ripple and others moved quickly to freeze or restrict access to about $3 billion in stolen assets; Coinbase blocked transfers to compromised wallets and offered Binance direct assistance.

The hack could even help shape the next phase of the crypto infrastructure trade. The new winners may not just be the businesses with the most users, volume or assets under custody, but the ones that can also demonstrate they're able to protect those assets under extreme duress.

"Which company has the resources to invest in cybersecurity and protect customer funds? For a smaller company, it could be much harder because once these crypto companies become infrastructure plays, providing the well for tokenization … they need to have the compliance and control in place so that a similar hacking will not happen or can be minimized," Owen Lau, senior analyst at ClearStreet, told CNBC. "Larger companies have more resources that could give them some advantage."

While institutional investors have increasingly embraced digital assets, broader adoption depends on confidence that those assets can be securely held, accessed and transferred. The Binance breach highlights the importance of that infrastructure, and could increase demand for specialized custodians and platforms with established asset-protection capabilities.

Coinbase has operated a dedicated institutional custody business since 2018. Circle entered the custody business this year, after the U.S. Office of the Comptroller of the Currency granted it approval to operate as a trust bank. In the past few years, Ripple and Kraken have also launched custody businesses.

Still, smaller players compete in the space as well. Small cap crypto exchange stock Gemini Space Station has offered custody services since 2019. BitGo Holdings, founded in 2013, is one of the earliest players in institutional crypto custody and infrastructure, with a reputation for security and serving major institutions.

The shift could also drive consolidation as companies look to buy capabilities, deepen their infrastructure offerings and achieve the scale needed to compete, Lau said.

The more consequential structural shift may be the separation of custody from execution, according to Morgan Stanley.

"Crypto exchanges have historically combined trading, custody, and settlement within vertically integrated platforms, concentrating operational risk. Repeated security breaches could encourage investors to move assets toward independent custody while continuing to trade on CEXs," the firm's analyst Felix Stratmann said in a note Monday," referring to centralized cryptocurrency exchanges.

"This would preserve the role of centralized exchanges as liquidity and execution venues, while potentially benefiting regulated custodians, including banks, as institutional investors place greater emphasis on asset segregation, operational resilience, and investor protection," Stratmann said.

#2838. Poll: Do you find the Brown-Tatum drama narrative interesting?

I feel compelled to ask, do you find this narrative interesting?

I'll start here: I totally understand why this is a thing. The NBA has become a 24-hour, 365-day entertainment vehicle that many of us consume obsessively. That reality is driven as much by drama between personalities, both real and manufactured, as by what actually takes place on the court. Brown and Tatum played together for nine seasons, their entire careers overlapping for all but one year, for a club perennially in contention which won one NBA title. If there was a fallout between them, and there seems like there's definitely at least something there, that's a story. If Brown had a disagreement with how he was treated by Celtics management, that's a topic worth exploring. We've hit on those points on this very website (thanks for reading, tell your friends!). If you have lamb chops baking in the oven today and popcorn ready for your viewing experience tonight, cool. I get it.

Still, I can't find myself really caring about it. Whatever happened, it all happened behind the scenes. There was no public infighting or drawn-out trade demand. The Jaylen Brown trade came completely out of left field like a flash of lightning. What Brown said the other day almost exactly aligns with my thinking on the matter:

In today's NBA, guys change teams all the time. Plenty of breakups have been a lot messier than this one, including in Philadelphia. I'll chuckle when someone makes a clever joke about everything, but beyond that, I can't find myself getting invested in the whole thing. It's Philadelphia vs. Boston. That's more than enough for me.

#2836. Vikings' Kirk Cousins deal might doom NFC West: NFL trade deadline winners, losers

Another eventful NFL trade deadline didn't disappoint on the final day the league's teams could swap players ahead of the 4 p.m. ET deadline on Tuesday, Nov. 10.

The most impactful move of the final week came down with little more than an hour to spare, the Las Vegas Raiders agreeing to send 38-year-old QB Kirk Cousins back to the Minnesota Vikings for a third-round pick in the 2027 NFL draft. The deal materialized not 24 hours after the Vikes were embarrassed in a 23-6 loss to the Buffalo Bills on "Monday Night Football," when Minnesota's offense couldn't find the end zone and struggled to simply get first downs.

But the Vikings weren't the only team that's been busy. Here's a fuller breakdown of this year's trade deadline winners and losers.

He leaves the rebuilding Raiders, his tutelage of No. 1 pick Fernando Mendoza complete, in order to return to Minnesota and pursue the ring that's eluded him throughout his 15-year career − six of those seasons spent in the Twin Cities. Cousins, who had 20 TD passes in games with the Silver and Black, is playing his best football since 2023, when what had been his presumed final season in Minnesota was cut short by an Achilles injury. Pretty cool that Cousins, who wasn't enthused about leaving for Atlanta in 2024, gets to return and maybe complete some unfinished business.

In a stacked division where every other team has bona fide stud at quarterback, the Vikes at least upgrade significantly at the position by bringing Cousins back in light of the failed Kyler Murray experiment. Armed with one of the elite defenses the league has seen in the 21st century, Minnesota at least gives itself a more viable Super Bowl shot than it had 24 hours ago.

"It's the best defense in the National Football League, and they have nothing to show for their work because of the ineptitude on the offensive side of the ball," ESPN analyst and former NFL defensive lineman Chris Canty said prior to the acquisition of Cousins.

Seems that work should now be more appropriately rewarded.

Their acquisiton of All-Pro DT Jeffery Simmons two weeks ago might very well wind up being the most impactful trade made over the past six weeks − particularly given how seamlessly Simmons seems to be integrating. Chicago has won both of its games since his arrival.

It was a painful (additional) reset, notably the decision to part with Simmons and SS Amani Hooker. But GM Mike Borgonzi and first-year coach Robert Saleh now have two first-round picks next year, two second-round picks next year and two first-round picks in 2028.

After trading WR A.J. Brown during the offseason, C/G Cam Jurgens last month and DT Jordan Davis yesterday, the Philadelphia Eagles' renowned personnel guru now owns 10 draft selections (first or second round) spread across the 2027-29 drafts. The Eagles may be down right now, but they won't be out for long.

He apparently wasn't the right QB for Minnesota − though it sure seems like the Vikings gave up on him prematurely and at a reduced rate (fifth-round pick). But McCarthy, who's now 2-0 as the New York Giants' starter, seems as if he landed in the right spot. And given Cousins is back with the Vikings, perhaps this is your classic win-win.

Both Keenan Allen (San Francisco 49ers) and Josh Downs (Kansas City Chiefs) were rescued from a sinking ship and now both get a shot to pursue a championship and, in Downs' case, maybe a sizable second contract as he plays out the remainder of his rookie deal with Patrick Mahomes throwing to him.

The Vikings' best player, who spent his first four NFL seasons catching passes from Cousins, once again becomes an impactful player after being relegated to JAG (just a guy) status the past two seasons.

It received an unmistakable message Monday, when the team aggressively obtained S Jessie Bates III and DTs Jordan Davis and Davon Godchaux to bolster a defense that recently lost S Eric Murray and DT DaVon Hamilton to season-ending injuries. The Jags, who should be on a glide path to a second straight AFC South crown and, quite possibly, the No. 1 seed in the conference, are undeniably in hot pursuit of the franchise's first Super Bowl berth in a year when the AFC seems fairly wide open.

Assuming the Raiders decide to play the No. 1 pick of the 2026 draft in Week 10, he'd be in line for his first pro start and just second appearance after getting some garbage-time snaps in Week 5.

Bates and Hooker both brought second-round picks to their former teams, a fairly phenomenal return given the position they play − and neither of them routinely man the ever-challenging slot corner role.

The Colts' Jonathan Taylor, Eagles' Saquon Barkley, Titans' Tony Pollard, Ravens' Derrick Henry and Jets' Breece Hall were among the backs whose names were floated as potential trade targets in recent weeks. Given the rebuild Indianapolis has initiated and Pollard's expiring deal, it seemed a veritable foregone conclusion both would change addresses. But it wasn't to be, with tailbacks apparently not as valuable as safeties.

Beginning your tenure as the Raiders' QB1 against the Seattle Seahawks' "Dark Side" defense, which isn't even three weeks removed from forcing three-time Super Bowl MVP Mahomes to endure the worst start of his NFL career? Good luck, pal.

Second-year GM James Gladstone gave up a second-round pick to effectively rent Bates, plus a first- and second-rounder for Davis, who most definitely isn't the second coming of Jeffery Simmons. It's time to win in Duval County. Now.

The offers must have really been underwhelming for him to remain stuck as the team's fourth or fifth option at wideout.

A team that has yet to win a game, whatever its reasons are, held on to players like OLBs Khalil Mack and Tuli Tuipulotu, WR Quentin Johnston and others rather than collecting needed assets going into 2027, when Mack and Tuipulotu are both out of contract. LB Daiyan Henley was the only Bolt to bolt, sent packing to the AFC West-leading Denver Broncos in a fortuitous twist for him.

With Puka Nacua set to join Davante Adams on injured reserve, a move for a veteran wide receiver seemed almost incumbent here. But Sean McVay and Les Snead will apparently roll with Konata Mumpfield and Tutu Atwell for better or, more likely, worse.

With the Vikings shored up by the Cousins acquisition, the reigning champion Seattle Seahawks and presumed 2026 champion Los Angeles Rams – both are currently 5-4, which is worse than the Green Bay Packers (6-3), last-place residents in the NFC North – can no longer bank on Minnesota's downward trajectory continuing. And, yes, while NFC North teams could attrit themselves to a degree in the latter part of the regular season, so will the Seahawks and Rams, who better get hot fast given all three of the conference's wild-card slots are currently projected to go to the NFC North.

He remains tethered to the rebooting Indianapolis Colts, which seems like an unfortunate fate for a 27-year-old running back who sounded Sunday as if he'd played his last snap for the franchise that drafted him in 2020. Taylor remains among the league's top backs but, reportedly, a second-round pick wasn't enough to liberate him even though his championship window almost certainly doesn't align with the organization's. Maybe the Colts move Taylor in the offseason but, for now, another season of his prime has essentially been wasted.

#2835. How much you can make in 2027 and still pay 0% capital gains tax

The IRS has released the capital gains tax brackets for 2027, which include a six-figure earnings limit for the 0% rate for married couples.

If you're eager to sell profitable brokerage account investments, higher income thresholds for the 0% rate could help reduce your tax bill, experts say.

"The 0% capital gains bracket is easily one of the most overlooked strategies for tax-free income," said Stephen Maggard, a certified financial planner with Abacus Planning Group in Columbia, South Carolina.

But it's important to know how the tax brackets work and the possible trade-offs before selling investments.

Despite recent stock market volatility, many investors have portfolio gains after years of market growth. As of market close Nov. 9, the S&P 500 was up about 10% year-to-date. The index also returned more than 16% in 2025, and over 23% in 2024.

If you're planning to leverage the 0% capital gains bracket in 2027 to sell or rebalance brokerage account assets, here are some key things to know.

The capital gains brackets — with rates of 0%, 15% or 20% — apply to assets owned for more than one year, known as long-term capital gains. By contrast, investments held for one year or less are short-term gains, subject to regular income tax rates.

Your rate depends on taxable income, which you calculate by subtracting the greater of the standard or itemized deductions from your adjusted gross income, or AGI. Taxable income also includes any gains from investments that year.

The standard deduction will rise to $16,600 for single taxpayers and $33,200 for married couples in 2027.

For example, let's say a married couple's AGI is $130,000 for 2027, including capital gains. After subtracting the $33,200 standard deduction, their taxable income would be $96,800, which falls below the 0% threshold.

However, taxable income should include mutual fund distributions, which can "dramatically alter year-end planning projections" and increase taxable income, according to CFP Jim Guarino, managing director at Baker Newman Noyes in Woburn, Massachusetts. He is also a certified public accountant.

If your federal taxable income is above the 0% threshold, you'll pay 15% on the portion of investment gains that exceed the 0% limit, up to that bracket's cap. Gains above that may be taxed at 20%.

Also, the 0% capital gains bracket applies to federal taxes, but your profits could still be subject to state-level taxes, experts say.

As income limits rise for the 0% capital gains bracket due to IRS inflation adjustments, more investors could have tax savings opportunities, experts say.

"There is tons of planning to be done here," especially for early retirees with lower income, according to CFP Dinon Hughes, a partner with Nvest Financial in Portsmouth, New Hampshire.

One strategy, known as "tax gain harvesting," is selling profitable brokerage account assets during lower-income years to leverage the 0% bracket, he said. Keep in mind, these sales stack on top of your other earnings, which could bump taxable income above the 0% threshold.

You can also sell the asset and immediately rebuy it to "reset the cost basis," or original purchase price, said CFP Charles Sachs, chief investment officer of Imperio Wealth Advisors in Coral Gables, Florida.

By resetting the cost basis to current market value, you can reduce future capital gains to trim your tax bill.

When selling assets at a loss, the "wash sale rule" blocks tax-loss harvesting when you rebuy a "substantially identical" asset within 30 days before or after the sale. But the same rule doesn't apply when harvesting gains.

Managing capital gains is only one part of yearly tax planning, according to Guarino with Baker Newman Noyes.

Before selling investments, you should know how boosting AGI could impact your broader financial plan, including income phase-outs for various tax breaks, he said.

For example, modified adjusted gross income can impact a range of tax benefits, such as Affordable Care Act health insurance premium subsidies, which can disappear once earnings exceed the federal limits by even a single dollar.

Another example is President Donald Trump's temporary $6,000 senior "bonus" deduction, which is worth up to $12,000 for married couples per year through 2028.

#2834. ‘Hear me out’… Kevin Garnett tells Stephen A. Smith to unite with LeBron James to speak to Donald Trump

NBA legend Kevin Garnett has told Stephen A. Smith to squash his beef with LeBron James.

18 months ago LeBron James confronted Stephen A. Smith on the basketball court over comments the ESPN analyst made over his son Bronny James.

But on a new episode of the KG & Stephen A Smith Show, the topic of LeBron James was raised, and he said he was reluctant to make the first move.
